Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. I hope I'm not wasting your time, but, um, I guess I came in because I barely sleep right anymore. Um... no, I don't think I have. I mean, I still really like the stuff I usually do. Like, if I'm just hanging out at home, I still enjoy playing video games or watching a show. I still want to do fun things.

Um... well, I really try not to dwell on it too much, you know? I want to focus on moving forward and doing better. So... I wouldn't say it's all the time.

Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm actively running away from things. I'm just trying to be smart about what I expose myself to, you know? 

Like, I definitely won't go near the coast trails right now. But I think taking a break from that specific spot is just a good, practical idea... right? And, um... my hiking buddies have been texting me a lot to go out, and I guess I kind of... dodge their messages. I feel really bad about it, and I don't want you to think I'm a bad friend or being anti-social or anything like that. 

It's just... even when they just talk about going out there, I get this feeling where my chest tightens up a bit. It's nothing crazy, just a little uncomfortable. So I just kind of avoid the topic and stay away from those trails so I can stay positive and keep doing well. That's a normal way to pace myself for now, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm making the right choices to get past this.

Like, I figured I just needed to shake it off and get back to work. I didn't want to be a complainer or anything. Is four months a long time for something like this? I hope I didn't wait too long to come in, I just really wanted to handle it myself so I wouldn't be a bother.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people, places, or activities that remind you of the accident on the bluff?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I have been, a little bit. I mean, it's not like I'm hiding in my house all day or anything like that. I still go to work and everything. But, um... I guess I just don't really go near the coast trails anymore. It's probably silly, right? I just... I don't know, I'd rather just avoid it so I don't get all worked up over nothing. \n\nAnd, um, I guess I've been kind of dodging texts from my hiking buddies, too. They're really great guys, and I feel terrible ignoring them, I really do. I just... even when they text about going out there, my chest gets all tight and I feel like I can't breathe right. I don't want to bring them down or ruin their fun, so it's just easier to pretend I'm busy. Is that... is that a normal way to react? I really hope I'm not being too dramatic about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these memories and dreams about the accident are significantly affecting your daily life and causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I really don't want to sound like I'm just making excuses or complaining. I know I should be able to handle things better than this. But... I guess it is affecting my life a little bit. \n\nI've been trying so hard to just push through and do my job, but I actually... um, I messed up recently. I missed some really important notes in the CRM system, and it caused a pretty bad call escalation. My supervisor had to write me up for it. I feel awful about it, I really do. I've been working so hard because I wanted to move up toward team lead, and now I'm just so worried I completely blew my chance. I just couldn't focus that day. Does that make me sound like a terrible employee? I really am trying my best, I promise.\n\nAnd, um... outside of work, I've also kind of fallen behind on studying for my GED. I'm just so exhausted all the time from waking up sweating, and... well, since I've been avoiding my hiking friends like I mentioned, I just end up isolating myself and not getting anything done. I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't let it get in the way of my goals like this, right? I hope you don't think less of me for letting things slip.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think I have. I mean, I still really like the stuff I usually do. Like, if I'm just hanging out at home, I still enjoy playing video games or watching a show. I still want to do fun things. \n\nI know I said earlier that I've been avoiding my hiking buddies, but... I really do still care about them and I want to hang out. It's just the trail stuff that makes me panic, not that I don't like them or don't like being active anymore. I still have the interest, it's just hard to actually do it sometimes with how tired I am. \n\nSo, yeah, I think my interests are still all there. Is... is that a good sign? I hope that means I'm holding up okay, right? I'm really trying not to let this ruin everything I like.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible for the accident on the bluff in any way?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I know it's not productive. But... yeah, I guess I do feel a little guilty. \n\nI just... I keep thinking about how stupid and careless it was for me to be out there on the bluff all by myself. I should have known better, right? It was my own fault for putting myself in that situation. And now... um, I don't know, it just feels like if I let my guard down anywhere, something bad is going to happen again. Like I can't trust myself to be safe. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I shouldn't beat myself up over it. I'm really trying to be more positive, I promise. It's just hard to shake that feeling sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope you don't think I'm being too hard on myself.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself thinking about being guilty for the accident on the bluff?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try not to dwell on it too much, you know? I want to focus on moving forward and doing better. So... I wouldn't say it's all the time. \n\nI guess it just pops into my head every now and then, maybe a few times a week? Usually when I'm already feeling stressed or if I'm having a hard time falling asleep. But I really try to push those thoughts away as soon as they come up because I know it's not helpful to just sit there and feel sorry for myself. \n\nI'm doing my best to stay positive and not let it consume me. Is... is that the right way to handle it? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I really am trying to keep it under control.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find that you don't derive much pleasure from them?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I think I covered that a bit earlier, but... yeah, I'm still really enjoying the things I usually do. \n\nLike I said, I still love playing my games and watching my shows. And I definitely still want to go out and do things with my friends, even if the hiking trails are a bit much right now. I really don't think I've lost the, um, the spark for any of it. I'm still trying to have fun and be normal, you know? \n\nI really want to make sure I'm not letting this whole thing drag me down too much. I'm doing okay with keeping up my hobbies, right? I hope that shows I'm trying my best to stay on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being easily startled by small things, like a loud noise?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think I'm extra jumpy or anything like that. \n\nI mean, if a door slams really loud right next to me, I might flinch a little, but... that's just normal, right? Anybody would do that. I don't think I'm jumping out of my skin at every little sound or overreacting. I really try to keep my cool and just brush it off if something loud happens. \n\nI definitely don't want you to think I'm walking around on edge all the time. I'm doing pretty good at staying calm, I think. Does that sound like a normal reaction to you? I hope I'm handling that okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you have strong physical reactions, such as a racing heart or sweating, when you're reminded of the bluff accident?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, my heart doesn't go crazy or anything like that. \n\nIf I see a picture of a cliff or something, maybe I feel a little... I don't know, a tiny bit tense for a second? But I don't get all sweaty or feel like I can't breathe or anything extreme. I just kind of take a deep breath and move on. I really try to just stay grounded, you know? \n\nI definitely don't let my body overreact to things like that. I think I'm managing those physical feelings pretty well, right? I mean, a little tension is pretty normal, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to keep myself healthy.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific memories or reminders of the bluff accident that you try to avoid thinking about?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm actively running away from things. I'm just trying to be smart about what I expose myself to, you know? \n\nLike, I definitely won't go near the coast trails right now. But I think taking a break from that specific spot is just a good, practical idea... right? And, um... my hiking buddies have been texting me a lot to go out, and I guess I kind of... dodge their messages. I feel really bad about it, and I don't want you to think I'm a bad friend or being anti-social or anything like that. \n\nIt's just... even when they just talk about going out there, I get this feeling where my chest tightens up a bit. It's nothing crazy, just a little uncomfortable. So I just kind of avoid the topic and stay away from those trails so I can stay positive and keep doing well. That's a normal way to pace myself for now, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm making the right choices to get past this.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself constantly on the lookout for potential dangers or threats in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I wouldn't say that. I don't think I'm, like, looking over my shoulder all the time or acting paranoid. \n\nI mean, I work in customer service, so I'm talking to people all day... I kind of have to stay pretty relaxed and friendly, you know? I pay attention to what's going on around me, sure, but just the normal amount. Like, just being smart and aware. \n\nI definitely don't want you to think I'm out here acting crazy or treating everything like it's a threat. I think I'm doing a pretty good job of just living my life normally. Just being a regular, responsible guy... that's how I should be handling things, right? I hope that sounds okay to you.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having difficulty falling asleep or staying asleep due to being constantly alert or on guard?\n\nPatient: Um... well, my sleep hasn't been perfect lately, I guess. I mean, that's actually kind of why I decided to come in. \n\nI wouldn't say I'm, like, pacing the halls or staying up all night on guard or anything like that. But... sometimes I'll just lie there, and if it's raining or something, my mind just sort of wanders back to the bluff giving way. It's just a little hard to shut my brain off, you know? \n\nAnd then, when I finally do fall asleep, I've been having some dreams that make me wake up a bit sweaty. It's not every single night, and I'm still making it to my shifts at the call center every day! I just... I show up feeling like I barely slept at all sometimes. \n\nBut I'm still working hard and getting by. I mean, it's pretty normal to have a few bad nights of sleep after something stressful happens, right? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get my rest back on track. I don't want you to think I'm falling apart or anything.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find very enjoyable, but now find that you have little to no interest in doing?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really.
